=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is an N \times N grid. Takahashi wants to color each cell black or white so that all of the following conditions are satisfied:

- For every row, the following condition holds:

- There exists an integer i\ (0\leq i\leq N) such that the leftmost i cells are colored black, and the rest are colored white.

- For every column, the following condition holds:

- There exists an integer i\ (0\leq i\leq N) such that the topmost i cells are colored black, and the rest are colored white.


Out of these N^2 cells, M of them have already been colored. Among them, the i-th one is at the X_i-th row from the top and the Y_i-th column from the left, and it is colored black if C_i is B and white if C_i is W.
Determine whether he can color the remaining uncolored N^2 - M cells so that all the conditions are satisfied.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
X_1 Y_1 C_1
\vdots
X_M Y_M C_M

Output

If it is possible to satisfy the conditions, print Yes; otherwise, print No.

Constraints


- 1\leq N\leq 10^9
- 1\leq M\leq \min(N^2,2\times 10^5)
- 1\leq X_i,Y_i\leq N
- (X_i,Y_i)\neq (X_j,Y_j)\ (i\neq j)
- C_i is B or W.
- All input numbers are integers.

Sample Input 1

4 3
4 1 B
3 2 W
1 3 B

Sample Output 1

Yes

For example, one can color the grid as in the following figure to satisfy the conditions. The cells already colored are surrounded by red borders.

Sample Input 2

2 2
1 2 W
2 2 B

Sample Output 2

No

No matter how the remaining two cells are colored, the conditions cannot be satisfied.

Sample Input 3

1 1
1 1 W

Sample Output 3

Yes

Sample Input 4

2289 10
1700 1083 W
528 967 B
1789 211 W
518 1708 W
1036 779 B
136 657 B
759 1497 B
902 1309 B
1814 712 B
936 763 B

Sample Output 4

No

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

So algorithm: collect for each row x:
- max_y_B[x] = max y among B cells in row
- min_y_W[x] = min y among W cells in row
Then L=max_y_B, U=min_y_W - 1 (if no W then N). If L > U => No.

Then sort rows with constraints by x. Traverse increasing x, maintain min_U_so_far = minimum U among rows seen so far. For current row, if min_U_so_far < L_current => No. Then update min_U_so_far = min(min_U_so_far, U_current). At end Yes.

Implementation plan:
def solve():
    import sys
    it = iter(sys.stdin.buffer.read().split())
    N = int(next(it)); M = int(next(it))
    rows = {}  # x -> [maxB, minW] maybe initialize [0, N+1]
    for _ in range(M):
        x = int(next(it)); y = int(next(it)); c = next(it).decode()
        if x not in rows: rows[x] = [0, N+1]
        if c == 'B':
            if y > rows[x][0]: rows[x][0] = y
        else:
            if y < rows[x][1]: rows[x][1] = y
    # But for rows with no constraints? not in dict; no need.
    items = []
    for x, (maxB, minW) in rows.items():
        L = maxB
        U = minW - 1  # if minW=N+1 => U=N
        if L > U:
            print("No"); return
        items.append((x, L, U))
    items.sort()
    minU = N + 1  # or 10**18; since U<=N, minU can be N+1
    for x, L, U in items:
        if minU < L:
            print("No"); return
        if U < minU:
            minU = U
    print("Yes")

Need be careful: minU initial should be N+1, because no previous constrained row. If current L can be up to N, minU=N+1 >= L. Good.
But what about previous unconstrained rows with U=N? They don't lower minU below N. If no previous constrained, minU=N+1. If previous constrained row has U=N, minU=N. If current L=N, minU=N >= L okay. If previous constrained row U=N-1 and current L=N, no. Good.

Potential issue: If there are rows with constraints but not all rows, and a row has no constraints but lies between constrained rows; minU should include unconstrained row's U=N? Since N >= any L, doesn't affect. Good.

Let's think about edge cases:
- N=1. M<=1. If cell B: row L=1,U=1 ok. If W: L=0,U=0 ok. Yes.
- Multiple cells same row: e.g. row x has B at y=5 and W at y=3. L=5,U=2 => No. Indeed row cannot have black prefix length >=5 and <3. Good.

Implementation:
import sys
def solve():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data: return
    N = int(data[0]); M = int(data[1])
    rows = {}
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(M):
        x = int(data[idx]); y = int(data[idx+1]); c = data[idx+2]; idx += 3
        if x in rows:
            arr = rows[x]
            if c == b'B':
                if y > arr[0]: arr[0] = y
            else:
                if y < arr[1]: arr[1] = y
        else:
            if c == b'B':
                rows[x] = [y, N+1]
            else:
                rows[x] = [0, y]
    items = []
    for x, (maxB, minW) in rows.items():
        L = maxB
        U = minW - 1
        if L > U:
            print("No"); return
        items.append((x, L, U))
    items.sort(key=lambda t: t[0])
    minU = N + 1
    for _, L, U in items:
        if minU < L:
            print("No"); return
        if U < minU:
            minU = U
    print("Yes")
if __name__ == "__main__": solve()
